Fig. 7.
Readability for
{\tt CCv}

A.1.
Readability and Visibility for
{\tt CCv}

For
X={\tt CCv}
,
{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is defined as the set of all transactions
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
s.t.

cond1

there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
such that

t^{\prime}~(\mathsf{CO}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}})^{+}~t^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{CO}~t
. If we have this
t^{\prime\prime}
, allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CCvcycle}
.

cond2

If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(
t^{\prime}
also writes on some
y\neq x
), there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
).
Having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
) results in
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t^{\prime\prime}
(wrt
x
) and
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t^{\prime}
(wrt
y
), and
\mathsf{CCvcycle}
.

cond3a

If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(
t^{\prime}
also writes on some
y\neq x
), there is no transaction

t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) and

t^{\prime\prime}~(\mathsf{CO}~\cup~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}})^{+}t^{\prime}
. Having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
along with

t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
) results in
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t^{\prime\prime}
(wrt
y
) and
\mathsf{CCvcycle}
.

cond3b

There are no transactions
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(for
y\neq x
) such that

t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) and
t_{1}~(\mathsf{CO}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}})^{+}~t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t^{\prime}
. Having such
t_{1},t_{2}
, along with
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t_{1}~({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}\cup\mathsf{CO})^{+}~t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t
. With
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
, we get
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t_{1}
and
\mathsf{CCvcycle}
.

cond4

If
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{rd},y},t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(for some
y\neq x
), there are no transactions
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
such that

t_{1}~(\mathsf{CO}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}})^{+}~t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t
and
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
). If we have this, allowing

t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t_{1}
, resulting
in
\mathsf{CCvcycle}
.

Figure
7
explains cycles created after violating conditions
\tt{cond1}-\tt{cond4}
.

\tt{cond3}
covers
\tt{cond3a}
and
\tt{cond3b}
.
For
\tt{cond3a}
,
t_{2}
coincides with
t^{\prime}
.

After adding
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
, we must check that
there are no consistency violations.
The check set
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is defined as the set of transactions which turn “sensitive”
on adding the new edge
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
.
Unless appropriate edges are added involving
these sensitive transactions, we may get consistency violating cycles
in the resultant trace.
Let
\tau^{tt^{\prime}}
denote the trace obtained by adding the new transaction
t
and the edge
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
to trace
\tau
. Now, we
identify
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
and the edges which must be added to
\tau^{tt^{\prime}}
to obtain a consistent extended trace.

For
X={\tt CCv},{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is classified into three categories.

(a)

The first kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are

\{t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)\mid t^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{CO}~t\}
.
Then we add from each
t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
a
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
to
t^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

(b)

The second kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) when
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
. Then we add
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t_{1}
to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

(c)

The third kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are

t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t^{\prime}

and we have
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) for some
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
. In this case, we add
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}~t_{1}
to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

Lemma A.1.

Given trace
\tau
, a transaction
t
and a variable
x
, we can construct the sets
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
,
{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,x)
in polynomial time.

Proof.

We prove that set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
can be computed in polynomial time (
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{3})
time), by designing an algorithm to generate set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
. The algorithm consists of the following steps:

(i)

First we compute the transitive closure of the relations

[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]
and
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]
, i.e, we compute
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}
and
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}
. We can use the Floyd-Warshall algorithm
(
Cormen
et al. 2009
)
to compute the transitive closure. This will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{3})
time.

(ii)

We compute the set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}\mid t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t\}
. This takes
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

(iii)

For each transaction
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
, we perform following checks:

–

Check
\mathtt{cond1:}
Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}

with
t^{\prime}

[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}

t^{\prime\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond2}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond2:}
Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
and
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond3:}

We first check case a of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
, and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to case two of
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

Next, we check case b of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
If there are no such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond4}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond4:}

Check whether there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
,
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
then
t^{\prime}\notin

{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
If there are no such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
then add
t^{\prime}
to
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

Similarly, we can compute
{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,x)
in polynomial time. This completes the proof.
∎

A.2.
Properties of Trace semantics under
{\tt CCv}

Lemma A.2.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CCv}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t)}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CCv}
.

Proof.

The proof follows trivially since we do not change the reads from and conflict relations and the transaction
t
added to the partial order has no successors. Thus, if
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CCv}
,
so does
\tau_{2}
.
∎

Lemma A.3.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CCv}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CCv}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\rangle
,
\tau_{2}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
, and
ev
=
r(p,t,x,v)
be a read event in transaction
t\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
. Suppose
\tau_{2}\nvDash{\tt CCv}
, and that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
is cyclic. Since
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CCv}
, and
t
has no outgoing edges,
on adding
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
,
it follows that the cycle in
\tau_{2}
is as a result
of the newly added
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
edges.

We prove that
on adding
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
,
such cycles are possible iff at least one of
{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
are true.

{\tt{cond1}}\vee\dots\vee{\tt{cond4}}
induces
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cyclicity

This direction is easy to see : assume
one of
{\tt{cond1}},\dots,{\tt{cond4}}
are true; then, as already
argued in the main paper, we will get a
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cycle on adding the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge from
t^{\prime}
to
t
and we are done.

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cyclicity implies
{\tt{cond1}}\vee\dots\vee{\tt{cond4}}

For the converse direction, assume that we add the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge from
t^{\prime}
to
t
and obtain a
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cycle in
\tau_{2}
. We now argue that this cycle has been formed because one of

{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
are true.

First of all, note that
t
has no outgoing edges in
\tau_{2}
, since
it is the current transaction being executed. Also,
we know that
\tau_{1}
has no
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}
cycles.
Thus, the cyclicity of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
is induced by the
newly added
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge as well as the newly added

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
edges. Note that adding the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge to
\tau_{1}
does not induce any cycle since
t
has no outgoing edges. Lets analyze the

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
edges added which induce cycles, and argue that
one of
{\tt{cond1}},\dots,{\tt{cond4}}
will be true.

(1)

We add
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
edges from
t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{1},t,x)
to
t^{\prime}
.
For these edges to induce a cycle, we should have a path from
t^{\prime}
to
t^{\prime\prime}
in
\tau_{1}
. That is, we have
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}
. This is captured by
{\tt{cond1}}
.

(2)

Consider
y\neq x
. If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
,
and we have
t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}^{y}t
. Then we add

(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{y}_{2}
. To get a cycle, we need a path from

t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
.

•

If
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
, then we add
(t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x}_{2}
, resulting in a cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond2}}
.

•

If we have a path

t^{\prime\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime}
, then we get a cycle again. This is handled by
{\tt{cond3}}
.

•

As a last case, to obtain a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
,
assume there is a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t
in
\tau_{1}
, and let
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
be the last transaction writing to
x
in this path. Note that this will induce a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
: the path from
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
comes by the
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x}_{2}
edge added from
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
since
we have
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}^{x}
. Once we get this path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
, we again have the path we were looking for to get the cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond4}}
.

Thus, we have shown that obtaining a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
is covered
by the conditions
{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
, and hence,
a
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cycle.

(3)

Consider
y\neq x
. If
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
such that
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
,
and we have
t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}^{y}t
. Then we add

(t_{4},t^{\prime\prime\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{y}_{2}
. To get a cycle, we need a path from

t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t_{4}
.
If we have a path

t^{\prime\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{+}t_{4}
, then we get a cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond3}}
.

Thus, we can think of
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
cycles as a result of the forbidden patterns described in
{\tt{cond}_{i}}

1\leq i\leq 4
. By construction of
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
, we ensure
\neg{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,\neg{\tt{cond}_{4}}
. Hence,

\tau_{2}\models{\tt CCv}
.
∎

Define
\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}
as the set of traces generated using
\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}
transitions, starting from an empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.

Consider a terminal trace
\tau
generated by
{\tt CCv}
DPOR algorithm starting from the empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.
That is, there is a sequence

\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}
with

\tau_{0}=\tau_{\emptyset}
,
and
\tau_{n}=\tau
.
Since
\tau_{\emptyset}
is a empty we have
\tau_{\emptyset}\models{\tt CCv}
,
it follows by
Lemmas
A.2
and
A.3
that
\tau\models{\tt CCv}
.

Hence, for each trace
\tau\in\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}
we have
\tau\models{\tt CCv}
.

A.3.
{\tt CCv}
DPOR Completeness

In this section, we show the completeness of the DPOR algorithm. More precisely, for the input program under
{\tt CCv}
for any terminating run
\rho\in Runs(\gamma)
and trace
\tau
s.t.
\rho\models\tau
,
we show that
\textsc{ExploreTraces}({\tt CCv},\tau_{\emptyset},\epsilon)
will produce a recursive visit
\textsc{ExploreTraces}({\tt CCv},\tau^{\prime},\pi)
for some terminal
\tau^{\prime}
, and
\pi
where
\tau^{\prime}=\tau
. First, we give some definitions and auxiliary lemmas.

Let
\pi=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
be an observation sequence where
\alpha_{t}=\mathsf{begin}(p,t)\dots

\mathtt{end}
(p,t)
.

\alpha_{t}
is called an
observable
, and is a sequence
of events from transaction
t
.
Given
\pi
and a trace
\tau
, we define

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi
to represent a sequence

\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{1}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{2}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\cdot\cdot\cdot\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{n}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}
, where

\tau_{0}=\tau
and

\pi=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
. Moreover, we define
\pi(\tau):=\tau_{n}
.

\tau
is
terminal
if there is no event left to execute
i.e

{\tt succ}\!\left(\tau\right)=\emptyset
.
We define
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi
to say that
(i)

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi
and
(ii)

\pi(\tau)
is terminal.

Definition A.4.

(
p
-free and
t
-free observation sequences)
For a process
p\in\mathbf{P}
, we say that an observation sequence
\pi
is
p
-free if all observables
\alpha_{t}
in
\pi
pertain to
transactions
t
not in
p
.
For a transaction
t
issued in
p
we say that
\pi
is
t
-free if
\pi
is
p
-free.

Definition A.5.

(Independent Observables)
For observables
\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we write
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
to represent they are independent.
This means

(i)

t_{1},t_{2}
are transactions issued in different processes, that is,

t_{1}
is issed in
p_{1}
,
t_{2}
is issued in
p_{2}
, with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
,

(ii)
no read event
r(p_{2},t_{2},x,v)
of transaction
t_{2}
reads from
t_{1}
, and

(iii)
no read event
r(p_{1},t_{1},x,v)
of transaction
t_{1}
reads from
t_{2}
. Thus,
\neg(\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}})
if either
t_{1},t_{2}
are issued in the same process, or there is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
relation between
t_{1},t_{2}
. That is,
\neg(\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}})
iff
t_{1}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t_{2}
or
t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t_{1}
.

Definition A.6.

(Independent Observation Sequences)
Observation sequences
\pi^{1},\pi^{2}
are called independent written
\pi^{1}\sim\pi^{2}
if there are observables
\alpha_{t_{1}}
,
\alpha_{t_{2}}
, and observation sequences
\pi^{\prime}

and
\pi^{\prime\prime}
such that
\pi^{1}=\pi^{\prime}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}
,

\pi^{2}=\pi^{\prime}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
.
In other words, we get
\pi^{2}
from
\pi^{1}
by swapping neighbouring independent observables corresponding to transactions
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
.

We use
\approx
to denote reflexive transitive closure of
\sim
.

Definition A.7.

(Equivalent Traces)
For traces
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\rangle
and
\tau_{2}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
, we say
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
are equivalent denoted
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
if
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
and for all
t_{1}
,
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{w,x}
for all variables
x
, we have
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x}
]
t_{2}
iff
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}^{x}
]
t_{2}
.

Lemma A.8.

((\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2})\wedge\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t})\Rightarrow(\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}\wedge(\alpha_{t}(\tau_{1})\equiv\alpha_{t}(\tau_{2})))
.

Proof.

Assume
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
for an observable
\alpha_{t}
, and

\tau_{1}=(\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1})
,

\tau_{2}=(\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2})
with
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
. Then we know that
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}
.

Since
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
, let
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau^{\prime}
.

\tau^{\prime}=(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime})
where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\{t\}
,

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup\{(t_{1},t)\mid t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
is in the same process as
t\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}
can contain
(t^{\prime},t)
for some

t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
, when
t^{\prime},t\in{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}^{w,x}
for some variable
x
, based
on the trace semantics. In particular if we have

t_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{2}
,
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
and
t_{3}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime})^{+}t_{2}
, then

t_{3}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{1}
.
4
4

4

We write
t~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x}~t^{\prime}
to denote that this
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
relation is added because of a read on
x
by a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
in the trace semantics.

Since
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
, for any transactions
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}]t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}^{x}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}]t^{\prime\prime}
for all variables
x
. In particular,
t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x}t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}^{x}t^{\prime\prime}
for all
x
.
Now, let us construct a trace
\tau^{\prime\prime}=(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime})
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime}
is the smallest set such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime}
and
whenever
t_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{2}
,
\tau_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
and
t_{3}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime})^{+}t_{2}
, then
t_{3}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime}}^{x}t_{1}
.

Since
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
for all
x
, and all
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime}
is the smallest extension of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}
based
on the trace semantics, along with the fact that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain for any two
transactions
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
,

t_{1}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}]t_{2}
iff
t_{1}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime\prime}]t_{2}
. This gives
\tau^{\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}
.

This also gives
\tau_{2}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
,
that is,
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
and indeed
\alpha_{t}(\tau_{1})=\tau^{\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}=\alpha_{t}(\tau_{2})
.

∎

Lemma A.9.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
and
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

Proof.

Let
\tau=\langle\mathcal{T},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}\rangle
be a trace and let
t_{1}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{1}
, and
t_{2}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{2}
, with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
. Assume
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
, with
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

We consider the following cases.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
. In this case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}
holds trivially.

(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)=(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}\rangle
, where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
.

\bullet

t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
,
t_{1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
and
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
.
We know that
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\rangle
, where

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1}\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
since there are no read events in
t_{1}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
since by the trace semantics, when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
remain same, there are no
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
edges to be added.

Consider
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.

Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{2}
,
reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get a sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.
Hence
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\{t_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{1}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{2}}\dots\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{n}})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i_{1}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i_{2}}\dots\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i_{n}})
.
Since
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
, for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},x_{i})
.

Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we know that for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},x_{i})
. It follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.
Define
\tau_{3}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
, where
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
. It follows that
\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\tau_{3}
,
\tau_{3}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3})=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau))
.

\bullet

t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
and
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
. Similar to previous case.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}\rangle
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
.
Since
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{1}
has read events.
Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{1}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables

x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get the sequence
\tau\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.
Let there be
m
read events
ev^{\prime}_{1}\dots ev^{\prime}_{m}
in transaction
t_{2}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
on variables

y_{1},\dots,y_{m}
.
Then we get the sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{m})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}}

Since
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
, for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
Since
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})
, for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},y_{j})
.
Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
,
it follows that, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
. Hence
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

Consider
\tau_{3}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}\rangle
. To show that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
,
we show that for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. We prove this using contradiction.
Assume that
\exists t^{\prime}_{i}
s.t.
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.

If
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, then
(1) there is a
t^{\prime}_{k}
such that
(i)
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
and (ii)
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
,
or (2) there are transactions
t_{3}
and
t_{4}
such that
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
,
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}_{3}t_{1}
,

t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
,
or (3) there are transactions
t_{3}
and
t_{4}
such that
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
,
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{1}
,

t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
.
or (4) there is a transaction
t_{3}
such that
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x,y}
,
t_{3}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}_{3}t_{1}
,
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x,y}
.

Consider case (1).
If (i) is true, that is,
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we also have
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
This, combined with
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
, gives according to the trace semantics,
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]t^{\prime}_{i}
. Going back to (ii),
we can have
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
only if we observed one of the following:

(a)

There is a path from
t^{\prime}_{i}
to
t^{\prime}_{k}
in
\tau
; that is,

t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
. This implies that
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
which is a contradiction.

(b)

There is no direct path from
t^{\prime}_{i}
to
t^{\prime}_{k}
, however,
there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}_{j},t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
such that, in
\tau
we had

t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{k}
and
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
, along with
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
,
t^{\prime\prime}_{l}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
. Let
ev^{\prime}_{j}=r(p_{2},t_{2},y_{j},v)
be the read event which reads from
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}
, justifying

t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
.

Since
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{k}
and
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
it follows that
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{k}
,
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
. By our assumption, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
and
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
, which gives us
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x_{i}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
(already observed in the para before (i)).

Thus we get
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{x_{i}}]t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
, which gives
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
. Hence
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},y_{j})
, a contradiction.

Consider case (2). If (2) is true, then we have transactions
t_{3}
and
t_{4}
such that
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
,
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}_{3}t_{1}
,

t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
.
We can have
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
only if we observe following:

(a)

We have
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t_{4}
in
\tau
.
This implies that
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
which is a contradiction.

(b)

We have either
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
or
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
due to the new
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
relations from reads in
t_{2}
in
\tau
.

Suppose we have
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{i}
and get
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t_{4}
due to transactions
t^{\prime\prime}_{j},t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
such that, in
\tau
we had

t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t_{4}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
, along with
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau

,t_{2},y_{j})
,
t^{\prime\prime}_{l}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
.
Let
ev^{\prime}_{j}=r(p_{2},t_{2},y_{j},v)
be the read event which reads from
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}
, justifying

t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau

,t_{2},y_{j})
.

Since
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t_{4}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}

it follows that
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime}_{k}
,
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
.
By our assumption, we have

t_{i}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x}_{i}~t_{1}
,
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}~t_{1}
,
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}

which gives us
t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{y}]t_{3}
.

Thus we get
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}^{y}]t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
, which gives
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
. Hence
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},y_{j})
, a contradiction.

A similar argument for other cases.

Thus, we have
\neg(t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k})
. This, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. Thus, we now have
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
.
It remains to show that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)}

Define
\tau_{4}{=}\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3}){=}\alpha_{t_{1}}.\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau){=}\langle\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}\rangle
. Recall that

\tau_{2}{=}
\alpha_{t_{2}}.\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
=
\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}\rangle
.

To show that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
, we show that
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
, and, for any two transactions

t^{\prime}_{k},t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4}
,
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
iff

t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
. Of these, trivially,

\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}
follow. Since
\alpha_{t_{2}}\sim\alpha_{t_{1}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
. It remains to prove the last condition.

Assume that
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x_{i}}_{1}]t_{1}\wedge t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{1},t_{1},x_{i})
,
where
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
. Then we have

t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x_{i}}_{1}]t_{1}\wedge t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
, and by the trace semantics, we have
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{1}]t^{\prime}_{i}
, and hence

t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}]t^{\prime}_{i}
. To obtain
\tau_{4}
, we execute
t_{2}
first obtaining
\tau_{3}
from
\tau
, and then
t_{1}
. We show that
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
, proving
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}\Rightarrow t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
.

If we have
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, then we are done since we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
, hence

t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, thereby obtaining

t^{\prime}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}t^{\prime}_{i}
, and hence
t^{\prime}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}t^{\prime}_{i}
.

Assume
t^{\prime}_{k}\notin{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.
Assume that on executing
t_{2}
from
\tau
, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{l}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
, and let
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y_{j}}_{3}]t_{2}
.
Then by the trace semantics,
t^{\prime\prime}_{l}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}]t^{\prime\prime}_{j}
is a new edge which gets added.
When we execute
t_{1}
next, assume
t^{\prime\prime}_{m}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.
Since we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, we obtain
t^{\prime\prime}_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}t^{\prime}_{i}
.

By assumption,
t^{\prime}_{k}\neq t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
. However,
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.

t^{\prime}_{k}\notin{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
points to some transaction that
blocked the visibility of
t^{\prime}_{k}
in
\tau_{3}
, by happening after
t^{\prime}_{k}
, and which is in
{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.

(1)

This blocking transaction could be
t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
. If this is the case, we have
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}_{m}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]t^{\prime}_{i}
, and hence
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}_{m}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]t^{\prime}_{i}
.

(2)

The other possibility is that we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}

happens before
t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
, and
t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
happens after
t^{\prime}_{k}
blocking

t^{\prime}_{k}
from being in
{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. That is,

t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}
and

t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
.
Then we obtain
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{l}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x_{j}}_{4}]t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{*}t^{\prime\prime}_{m}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]t^{\prime}_{i}
.

Thus, we obtain
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
as desired.

The converse direction, that is, whenever
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{4}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
, we also have
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{2}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{i}
is proved on similar lines.

∎

Lemma A.10.

If
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
,
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, and
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})
, then (i)
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})
and (ii)
(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau_{1})\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau_{2})
.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
A.8
and Lemma
A.9
.
∎

From Lemma
A.10
, we get following lemma.

Lemma A.11.

If
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\sim\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
, and
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
then
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}(\tau_{1})\equiv\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}(\tau_{2})
.

Lemma A.12.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
,
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
then
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
for some
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
where
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is
t
-free.

Consider observables
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\alpha_{t_{1}},\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
.
We write
\pi_{\tt{T}}\lessapprox\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
to represent that
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{0}}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{n}}}
.
In other words,
\pi_{\tt{T}}
occurs as a non-contiguous subsequence in
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
. For such a
\pi_{\tt{T}},\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
,
we define
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\oslash\pi_{\tt{T}}:=\pi_{\tt{T^{0}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\dots\pi_{\tt{T^{n}}}
.
Since elements of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
are distinct, operation
\oslash
is well defined.
Let
\pi_{\tt{T}}[i]
denote the
i
th observable
in the observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T}}
, and
let
|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
denote the number of observables
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Let
\alpha_{t}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[i]
for some
i:1\leq i\leq|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
.
We define
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
as a subsequence
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
such that
(i)
\alpha_{t}\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
,
(ii)
\alpha_{t_{j}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[j]\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
for some
j:1\leq j<i
iff there exists
k:j<k\leq i
such that
\alpha_{t_{k}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[k]\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
and
\neg(\alpha_{t_{j}}\sim\alpha_{t_{k}})
.
Thus,
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
consists of
\alpha_{t}
and all

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}
appearing before
\alpha_{t}
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
such that

t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t
.

Lemma A.13.

If
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}=\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}\oslash\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
then
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
.

Proof.

The proof is trivial since we can always execute in order,
the
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}
predecessors of
\alpha_{t}
from
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
, then
\alpha_{t}
, then the observables
in
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
which are independent from
\alpha_{t}
, followed by the suffix of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
after
\alpha_{t}
.

∎

Lemma A.14.

If
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}_{T}
and
\alpha_{t}\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
then
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}=\tt{Pre(\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
.

Proof.

The proof is trivial once again, since all
the transactions which are
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t
are in the prefix
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
.
∎

Lemma A.15.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
.

Proof.

This simply follows from the fact that we can extend the observation sequence

\pi_{\tt{T}}
to obtain a terminal configuration, since the
{\tt CCv}
DPOR algorithm generates traces corresponding to terminating runs. ∎

Lemma A.16.

Consider a trace trace
\tau
such that
\tau\models{\tt CCv}
,
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T}}\cdot\alpha_{t}
. Let each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
in
\alpha_{t}
read from some transaction
t_{i}
, and let
\pi_{\tt{T}}
be
t
-free. Then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T}}
, where
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
is the same as
\alpha_{t}
, with the exception that the sources of its read events can be different. That is,
each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
can read from some transaction
t^{\prime}_{i}\neq t_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
and
\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{1}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{2}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{n}}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n+1}
, where
\tau_{0}=\tau
. Let
\tau_{i}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{i},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}\rangle
.
Let there be
m
read events in transaction
t
. Keeping in mind what we want to prove, where we want to execute
t
first followed by
\pi_{\tt{T}}
, and obtain an execution
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
, we do the following.

For each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
we define
t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x_{i}}
such that

(i)

t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
, and

(ii)
there is no
t^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
where
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{n}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{n}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{n}]t^{\prime\prime}_{i}
. Note that this is possible since

\pi_{\tt{T}}
is
t
-free, so all the observables
\alpha_{t_{i}}
occurring in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
are such that
t_{i}
is issued in a process other than that of
t
. Thus, when
\alpha_{t}
is enabled, we can choose any of the
writes done earlier, this fact is consistent with the
{\tt CCv}
semantics, since from Lemmas
A.2
-
A.3
we know
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CCv}
.
Since
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CCv}
such a
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
exists for each
ev_{i}
.

Next we define a sequence of traces which can give the execution

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
Define a sequence of traces
\tau^{\prime}_{0},\tau^{\prime}_{1},\dots\tau^{\prime}_{n}
where
\tau^{\prime}_{j}=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{j}\rangle
is such that

(1)

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,

(2)

\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for all
j:0\leq j\leq n
.

•

Define
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{i}\cup\{t\}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

Define
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}]t\mid t^{\prime}
is a transaction in
\tau
, in the same process as
t\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i:1\leq i\leq m}t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x_{i}}]t
(reads from relation corresponding to each read event
ev_{i}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
), and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j:1\leq j\leq m}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{j}
(each
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{j}
corresponds to the updated
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
relation because of the read transition
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime}_{j})}_{{\tt CCv}{-}{\tt tr}}
).

For
1\leq i\leq n
, we define
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i}
inductively as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}
and show that
\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
holds good.
First define
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{0}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}
. Then define
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i}
to be the coherence order corresponding to
\alpha_{t_{i}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i-1})
where
\tau^{\prime}_{i-1}=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{{i-1}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i-1}\rangle
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
.

Base case
. The base case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,
holds trivially, by construction.

Inductive hypothesis
. Assume that
\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for
0\leq j\leq i-1
.

We have to prove that

\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
.

(1)

If
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
and
t_{i+1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, then the proof holds trivially from the inductive hypothesis.

(2)

Consider now
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
. Consider a read event

ev^{i+1}=r(p,t_{i+1},x,v)\in\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
which was reading from
transaction
t_{x}
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
. That is, we had
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
. If
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, then we are done, since we can simply extend the run from the inductive hypothesis.

Assume otherwise. That is,
t_{x}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.

Since
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, we know that there are some blocking transactions in the new path which prevents
t_{x}
from being readable. Basically, we have the blocking transactions since
t
is moved before
\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

•

Consider
tr_{1}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,y)
for some variable
y
such that, in
\pi_{\tt{T}}.\alpha_{t}
, we had
t_{x}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}]^{*}tr_{1}
. This path
is possible since
\alpha_{t}
comes last in in
\pi_{\tt{T}}.\alpha_{t}
, after
\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
.

•

Consider
tr_{2}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
. Since
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, we have
tr_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x}_{i+1}]t_{x}
.
Now, in
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}.\pi_{\tt{T}}
, assume
tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime y}_{0}]t
,
such that we have a path from
tr_{3}
to
t_{x}
, as
tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}]^{*}tr_{2}
.

Hence it follows that
tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}]^{*}tr_{2}

[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{x}_{i+1}]t_{x}

[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}]^{*}tr_{1}
.
Hence we have
tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i+1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i+1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i+1}]^{+}tr_{1}
, i.e

tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{n}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{n}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{n}]^{+}tr_{1}
. This leads to the contradiction since
tr_{1}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,y)
and
tr_{3}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime y}_{0}]t
.

Hence,
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
and we can extend the run
from the inductive hypothesis obtaining
\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
,
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
,
for
0\leq j\leq i-1
,
and

\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
.

Now we prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i+1}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i+1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}
.
Assume we have
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i+1}
. We show that
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
. We have the following cases:

(i)

(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}
. From the inductive hypothesis it follows that
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i}
and hence in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.

(ii)

(tr1,tr2)\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}_{i}
and some read event
ev=r(p,t_{i+1},x,v)
from
\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
reads from
tr2
. That is,
tr1,tr2\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
and
tr1\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.
Assume
(tr1,tr2)\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
Since
(tr1,tr2)\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
, it follows that there exists
tr3\in{\tt{check}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
such that
tr2[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}^{\prime}_{i}]tr3
. This means
tr2\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, but

tr2\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
. However, as proved earlier, this leads to a contradiction.

Thus, we have shown that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}

is such that
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{i}
for all

0\leq i\leq n
. When
i=n
we obtain

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{n}
, or
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
∎

Definition A.17 (Linearization of a Trace).

A observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T}}
is a
linearization
of a trace
\tau=\langle\mathcal{T},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}\rangle
if
(i)

\pi_{\tt{T}}
has the same transactions as
\tau
and
(ii)

\pi_{\tt{T}}
follows the (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}})
relation.

We say that our DPOR algorithm
generates an observation sequence

\pi_{\tt{T}}
from state
\tau
where
\tau
is a trace
if
it invokes
ExploreTraces
with parameters
{\tt CCv},\tau,\pi^{\prime}
, where
\pi^{\prime}
is a
linearization
of
\tau
, and generates a sequence of recursive calls to
ExploreTraces
resulting in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Lemma A.18.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
then,
the DPOR algorithm generates
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
from state
\tau

for some
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Proof.

We use induction on
|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
. If
\tau
is
terminal
, then the proof is trivial.
Assume that we have
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
Assume that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
. It follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
.
Using Lemma
A.12
we get
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
, where
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is
t
-free.
We consider the following two cases:

•

In the first case, we assume that all read events in
t
read from
transactions in
\tau
. So in this case,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
can be empty.
Assume there are
n
read events in
t
and each read event
ev_{i}=r(p,t,x_{i},v)

reads from transactions
t_{i}\in\tau
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
.
In this case the DPOR algorithm will let each read event
ev_{i}
read from all possible transactions
t^{\prime}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x_{i})
, including
t_{i}
.

•

In the second case, assume that there exists at least one read event
ev^{\prime}=r(p,t,x,v)\in\alpha_{t}
which reads from a transaction
t^{\prime}\in\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
(hence,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is non empty).

From Lemma
A.16
, we know that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
.
From Lemma
A.15
, it follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
, for some

\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.
Hence there exists
\tau^{\prime}
such that

\tau^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)
and
\tau^{\prime}\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.

Since
|\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}|<|\pi_{\tt{T}}
, we can use the inductive hypothesis. It follows that the DPOR algorithm generates from state
\tau^{\prime}
,
the observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}
such that
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.

Let
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}.\alpha_{t}=\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}},\alpha_{t})}
. Then
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
implies that
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\lessapprox\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}
(by applying Lemma
A.14
).
Let
\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T}}\oslash(\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t})
.
By applying Lemma
A.13
, we get
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
Since
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
and

\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
,
by applying Lemma
A.10
, we get
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
Let
\tau^{\prime}=(\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t})(\tau)
.
Since
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
, we have
\tau^{\prime}\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
From inductive hypothesis it follows that
\tau^{\prime}
generates
\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
such that
\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.

In other words,
\tau
generates
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
where
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
.

∎
