Readability for
{\tt CC}

B.1.
Readability for
{\tt CC}

For
X={\tt CC}
,
{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is defined as the set of all transactions
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
s.t.

\tt{cond1}

there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
s.t.
t^{\prime}~\mathsf{CO}~t^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{CO}~t
in
\tau^{t}_{X}
.
Allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
) in the presence of such a
t^{\prime\prime}
gives
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}~t^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CCcycle}
.

\tt{cond3a}

In case
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(
t^{\prime}
also writes on some
y\neq x
) and
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{rd},y}
,
there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) and
t^{\prime\prime}~\mathsf{CO}~t^{\prime}
. Note that having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
, and
allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
)
results in
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}~t^{\prime\prime}
(wrt
y
) and
\mathsf{CCcycle}
.

\tt{cond3b}

if
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{rd},y}
(for some
y\neq x
) there are no transactions
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that

t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
),
t_{1}~\mathsf{CO}~t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t^{\prime}
. Assuming we have
this, then allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
) gives
t_{1}~\mathsf{CO}~t_{2}~\mathsf{CO}~t

and hence
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}~t_{1}
(wrt
y
) and
\mathsf{CCcycle}
.

Figure
8
explains cycles created after violating conditions
\tt{cond1}
and
\tt{cond4}
.

\tt{cond3}
covers
\tt{cond3a}
and
\tt{cond3b}
.
For
\tt{cond3a}
,
t_{2}
coincides with
t^{\prime}
.

Lemma B.1.

Given trace
\tau
, a transaction
t
and a variable
x
, we can construct the set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
in polynomial time.

Proof.

We prove that set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
can be computed in polynomial time, by designing an algorithm to generate set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
. The algorithm consists of the following steps:

(i)

First we compute the transitive closure of the relation

[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]
, i.e, we compute
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}
.
This will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{3})
time.

(ii)

We compute the set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}\mid t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t\}
. This takes
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

(iii)

For each transaction
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
, we perform following checks:

–

Check
\mathtt{cond1:}
Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}

with
t^{\prime}

[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}

t^{\prime\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond3:}

We first check case a of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
, and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to case two of
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

Next, we check case b of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
If there are no such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
then
t^{\prime}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

∎

B.2.
Properties of Trace Semantics

Lemma B.2.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CC}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t)}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CC}
.

Proof.

The proof follows trivially since we do not change the reads from relations, and the transaction
t
added has no successors in
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2})
.
Thus, if
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CC}
, so does
\tau_{2}
.
∎

Lemma B.3.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CC}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CC}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{1}\rangle
,

\tau_{2}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}\rangle
5
5

5

following trace semantics,
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}
relation will always be empty.
,and

ev
=
r(p,t,x,v)
be a read event in transaction
t\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
such that
ev
reads value of
x
from the transaction
t^{\prime}
.
Suppose
\tau_{2}\nvDash{\tt CC}
, and that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}
is cyclic.
Since
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CC}
, and
t
has no outgoing edges, just by adding
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
we can not get cycle in (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}
).
Now, suppose that are transactions
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{W,y},t_{2}
and
t_{3}
such that
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
]
+

t_{3}
and
t_{2}
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}^{y}
]
t_{3}
.
We need to show that in trace
\tau_{2}
, we have
\neg
(t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t_{1})
.
We consider the following cases:

\bullet

t_{3}=t
,
t_{2}=t^{\prime}
.
From the definition of
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}
,
we have
t_{2}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{3},x)
.
So we have
\neg
(t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}]^{+}t_{1})
.
Since
t
has no successor in
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2})
, it follows that in
\tau_{2}
we will have
\neg
(t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}]^{+}t_{1})
.
Thus,
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CC}
.

\bullet

t_{3}\neq t
,
t_{2}\neq t^{\prime}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]t
and
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
]
+

t_{3}
and
t_{2}
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}^{y}
]
t_{3}
, it follows that
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}
]
+

t_{3}
and
t_{2}
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}^{y}
]
t_{3}
.
Since
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CC}
, we have
\neg
(t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}]^{+}t_{1})
.
Since only new edge added in
\tau_{2}
is
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x}]t
, we will have
\neg
(t_{2}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}]^{+}t_{1})
in
\tau_{2}
.
Thus,
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CC}
is.

∎

Define
\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}
as the set of traces generated using
\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}
transitions, starting from an empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.
Consider a terminal trace
\tau
generated by
{\tt CC}
DPOR algorithm starting from the empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.
That is, there is a sequence

\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}
with

\tau_{0}=\tau_{\emptyset}
,
and
\tau_{n}=\tau
.
Since
\tau_{\emptyset}
is a empty we have
\tau_{\emptyset}\models{\tt CC}
,
it follows by
Lemmas
A.2
and
A.3
that
\tau\models{\tt CC}
.

Hence, for each trace
\tau\in\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}
we have
\tau\models{\tt CC}
.

B.3.
Completeness of
{\tt CC}
DPOR

Proof for completeness of DPOR for
{\tt CC}
follows the same schema as the completeness proof for
{\tt CC}
-DPOR. The only difference
is in Lemmas
A.9
and
A.16
.
We prove Lemmas
B.4
and
B.5
, which are the
{\tt CC}
counterpart of Lemmas
A.9
and
A.16
, respectively.

Lemma B.4.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
and
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

Proof.

Let
\tau=\langle\mathcal{T},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}\rangle
be a trace and let
t_{1}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{1}
, and
t_{2}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{2}
, with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
.
Assume
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
, with
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

We consider the following cases.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{r}
. In this case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}
holds trivially.

(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)=(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}\rangle
, where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
.

\bullet

t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{w}
,
t_{1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{r}
and
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}\cap\mathcal{T}^{w}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
.
We know that
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{1}\rangle
, where

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1}\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
since there are no read events in
t_{1}
.

Consider
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}\rangle
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.

Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{2}
,
reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get a sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.
Hence
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\{t_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{1}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{2}}\dots\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{n}})
.
Since
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
, for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},x_{i})
.

Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we know that for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},x_{i})
. It follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.
Define
\tau_{3}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}\rangle
, where
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
. It follows that
\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\tau_{3}
,
\tau_{3}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3})=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau))
.

\bullet

t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{w}
and
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}\cap\mathcal{T}^{w}
. Similar to previous case.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}\cap\mathcal{T}^{w}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\rangle
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})
. Since
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}
, we know that
t_{1}
has read events.
Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{1}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables

x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get the sequence
\tau\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.
Let there be
m
read events
ev^{\prime}_{1}\dots ev^{\prime}_{m}
in transaction
t_{2}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
on variables

y_{1},\dots,y_{m}
.
Then we get the sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{m})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}}

Since
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
, for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
Since
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})
, for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},y_{j})
.
Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
,
it follows that, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
. Hence
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

Consider
\tau_{3}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{3}\rangle
. To show that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
,
we show that for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. We prove this using contradiction.
Assume that
\exists t^{\prime}_{i}
s.t.
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.

If
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, then
there is a
t^{\prime}_{k}
such that
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
and
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
.

If
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we also have
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
Since new relations added in
\tau_{3}
are
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})\mid t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}\bigcup_{j:1\leq j\leq m}t^{\prime\prime}_{j}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y_{j}}]t_{2}
,
we can have
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
only if we there is a path from
t^{\prime}_{i}
to
t^{\prime}_{k}
in
\tau
; that is,

t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
.
This implies that
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
which is a contradiction.

Thus, we have
\neg(t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k})
. This, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. Thus, we now have
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
.
It remains to show that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)}

Define
\tau_{4}{=}\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3}){=}\alpha_{t_{1}}.\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau){=}\langle\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{4}\rangle
.

Recall that

\tau_{2}{=}

\alpha_{t_{2}}.\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
=
\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}\rangle
.

Since,
\alpha_{t_{2}}\sim\alpha_{t_{1}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
. It follows trivially that
\mathcal{T}_{4}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}
.
Since
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{4}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{2}
it follows that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

∎

Lemma B.5.

Consider a trace trace
\tau
such that
\tau\models{\tt CC}
,
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T}}\cdot\alpha_{t}
. Let each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
in
\alpha_{t}
read from some transaction
t_{i}
, and let
\pi_{\tt{T}}
be
t
-free. Then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T}}
, where
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
is the same as
\alpha_{t}
, with the exception that the sources of its read events can be different. That is,
each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
can read from some transaction
t^{\prime}_{i}\neq t_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
and
\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{1}}}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{2}}}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{n}}}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CC}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n+1}
, where
\tau_{0}=\tau
.
Let
\tau_{i}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{i},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}_{i}\rangle
.
Let there be
m
read events in transaction
t
. Keeping in mind what we want to prove, where we want to execute
t
first followed by
\pi_{\tt{T}}
, and obtain an execution
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
, we do the following.

For each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
we define
t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{w,x_{i}}
such that

(i)

t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
, and

(ii)
there is no
t^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
where
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{n}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{n}]t^{\prime\prime}_{i}
.
Note that this is possible since

\pi_{\tt{T}}
is
t
-free, so all the observables
\alpha_{t_{i}}
occurring in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
are such that
t_{i}
is issued in a process other than that of
t
.
Thus, when
\alpha_{t}
is enabled, we can choose any of the
writes done earlier, this fact is consistent with the
{\tt CC}
semantics, since from Lemmas
B.2
-
B.3
we know
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CC}
.
Since
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CC}
such a
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
exists for each
ev_{i}
.

Next we define a sequence of traces which can give the execution

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
Define a sequence of traces
\tau^{\prime}_{0},\tau^{\prime}_{1},\dots\tau^{\prime}_{n}
where
\tau^{\prime}_{j}=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}\rangle
6
6

6

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.5,0.06}\mathsf{OW}}
relation will be empty following trace semantics.
is such that

(1)

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,

(2)

\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for all
j:0\leq j\leq n
.

•

Define
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{i}\cup\{t\}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

Define
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}]t\mid t^{\prime}
is a transaction in
\tau
, in the same process as
t\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i:1\leq i\leq m}t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x_{i}}]t
(reads from relation corresponding to each read event
ev_{i}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
), and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
,

We show that
\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
holds good.

Base case
. The base case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,
holds trivially, by construction.

Inductive hypothesis
. Assume that
\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for
0\leq j\leq i-1
.

We have to prove that

\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
.

(1)

If
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{w}
and
t_{i+1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{r}
, then the proof holds trivially from the inductive hypothesis.

(2)

Consider now
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{r}
. Consider a read event

ev^{i+1}=r(p,t_{i+1},x,v)\in\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
which was reading from
transaction
t_{x}
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
. That is, we had
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
. If
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, then we are done, since we can simply extend the run from the inductive hypothesis.

Assume otherwise. That is,
t_{x}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.
Since
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, we know that there are some blocking transactions in the new path which prevents
t_{x}
from being readable. Basically, we have the blocking transactions since
t
is moved before
\pi_{\tt{T}}
. Consider
tr_{1}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.
Since
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
and
t_{x}

\notin

{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, we have
t_{x}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}]tr_{1}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
, and
t
has no successor in
\tau^{\prime}_{i}
, it follows that
t_{x}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}]tr_{1}
. This contradicts the fact that
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.

Thus, we have shown that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}

is such that
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{i}
for all

0\leq i\leq n
. When
i=n
we obtain

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{n}
, or
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
∎∎

Readability for

C.1.
Readability and Visibility for
{\tt CM}

Let
t
be a transaction in process
p
. For
X={\tt CM}
,
{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is defined as the set of all transactions
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
s.t.

\tt{cond1}

there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
such that

t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t
. Having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
and allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CMcycle}
.

\tt{cond2}

If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(for
y\neq x
), there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
).
Having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
x
) results in
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
,
t~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathsf{CMcycle}
.

\tt{cond3a}

If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(for
y\neq x
), there is no transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
). Having such a
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime\prime}
giving
\mathsf{CMcycle}
.

\tt{cond3b}

There are no transactions
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that

t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
), and
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
.
Having such
t_{1},t_{2}
with
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t
. With

t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
we get
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{1}
and
\mathsf{CMcycle}
.

\tt{cond4}

If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
(for
y\neq x
), there are no transactions
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
such that
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
),
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t
. If so, allowing
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
gives

t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
. Also,
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t,t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}
gives
t~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}
.
Now, we have
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}
obtaining
\mathsf{CMcycle}
.

Figure
9
explains cycles created after violating conditions
\tt{cond1}-\tt{cond4}
.

\tt{cond3}
covers
\tt{cond3a}
and
\tt{cond3b}
.
For
\tt{cond3a}
,
t_{2}
coincides with
t^{\prime}
.

After adding
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
, we must check that
there are no consistency violations.
The check set
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is defined as the set of transactions which turn “sensitive”
on adding the new edge
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
.
Unless appropriate edges are added involving
these sensitive transactions, we may get consistency violating cycles
in the resultant trace.
Let
\tau^{tt^{\prime}}
denote the trace obtained by adding the new transaction
t
and the edge
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
to trace
\tau
. Now, we
identify
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
and the edges which must be added to
\tau^{tt^{\prime}}
to obtain a consistent extended trace.

For
X={\tt CCv}
, let
X
-edge=
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathsf{CF}}
,
X
-rel=
\mathsf{CO}

and for
X={\tt CM}
, let
X
-edge=
X
-rel=
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
.

For
X={\tt CM}
,
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
is classified into three categories.

(a)

The first kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are

\{t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)\mid t^{\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t\}
.
Then we add from each
t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
a
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
to
t^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

(b)

The second kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) when
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
. Then we add
t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{1}
to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

(c)

The third kind of transactions in
{\tt{check}}(\tau^{t}_{X},t,x)
are

t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
such that
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}

and we have
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t
(wrt
y
) for some
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
. In this case, we add
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{1}
to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
. Adding the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
edges
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{1}
to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})

can result in
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{4}
for some
t_{3},t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
.
If
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}~t_{1}
and
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}~t
then we add
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{4}

to
\mathsf{extend}_{X}(\tau^{tt^{\prime}})
.

Lemma C.1.

Given trace
\tau
, a transaction
t
and a variable
x
, we can construct the sets
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
and
{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,x)
in polynomial time.

Proof.

Let
p
be process of
t
.
We prove that set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
can be computed in polynomial time (
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{3})
time), by designing an algorithm to generate set
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
. The algorithm consists of the following steps:

(i)

First we compute the transitive closure of the relations

[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}]
and
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]
, i.e, we compute
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{+}
and
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}]^{+}
.
This will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{3})
time.

(ii)

We compute the set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}\mid t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t\}
. This takes
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

(iii)

For each transaction
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
, we perform following checks:

–

Check
\mathtt{cond1:}
Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}

with
t^{\prime}

{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{+}

t^{\prime\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond2}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond2:}
Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
and
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond3:}

We first check case a of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there exists a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
, and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}]^{+}t^{\prime}
.
If there is no such
t^{\prime\prime}
then proceed to case two of
\mathtt{cond3}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|)
time.

Next, we check case b of
\mathtt{cond3}
. Check whether there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,y}
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{+}~t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
If there are no such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
then proceed to
\mathtt{cond4}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

–

Check
\mathtt{cond4:}

Check whether there are transactions
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}]t
,
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
,
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
and
t^{\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
If we find such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
then
t^{\prime}\notin

{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
If there are no such
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
then add
t^{\prime}
to
{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x)
.
This step will take
O(|\mathcal{T}|^{2})
time.

Similarly, we can compute
{\tt{check}}(\tau,t,x)
in polynomial time. This completes the proof.
∎

C.2.
Properties of the Trace Semantics

Lemma C.2.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CM}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t)}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CM}
.

Proof.

The proof follows trivially since we do not change the reads from and coherence order relations and the transaction
t
added to the partial order has no successors. Thus, if
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CM}
,
so does
\tau_{2}
.
∎

Lemma C.3.

If
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CM}
and
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
then
\tau_{2}\models{\tt CM}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\rangle
,
\tau_{2}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
, and
ev
=
r(p,t,x,v)
be a read event in transaction
t\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
. Suppose
\tau_{2}\nvDash{\tt CM}
, and that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
is cyclic. Since
\tau_{1}\models{\tt CM}
, and
t
has no outgoing edges in
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}})^{+}
,
on adding
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
,
it follows that the cycle in
\tau_{2}
is as a result
of the newly added
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}
edges.

We prove that
on adding
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
,
such cycles are possible iff at least one of
{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
are true.

{\tt{cond1}}\vee\dots\vee{\tt{cond4}}
induces
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cyclicity

This direction is easy to see : assume
one of
{\tt{cond1}},\dots,{\tt{cond4}}
are true; then, as already
argued in the main paper, we will get a
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cycle on adding the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge from
t^{\prime}
to
t
and we are done.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cyclicity implies
{\tt{cond1}}\vee\dots\vee{\tt{cond4}}

For the converse direction, assume that we add the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge from
t^{\prime}
to
t
and obtain a
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cycle in
\tau_{2}
. We now argue that this cycle has been formed because one of

{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
are true.

First of all, note that
t
has no outgoing edges in
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2})^{+}
in
\tau_{2}
, since
it is the current transaction being executed. Also,
we know that
\tau_{1}
has no
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}
cycles.
Thus, the cyclicity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
is induced by the
newly added
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge as well as the newly added

{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}
edges. Note that adding the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}
edge to
\tau_{1}
does not induce any cycle since
t
has no outgoing edges. Lets analyze the

{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}
edges added which induce cycles, and argue that
one of
{\tt{cond1}},\dots,{\tt{cond4}}
will be true.

(1)

We add
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}
edges from
t^{\prime\prime}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{1},t,x)
to
t^{\prime}
.
For these edges to induce a cycle, we should have a path from
t^{\prime}
to
t^{\prime\prime}
in
\tau_{1}
. That is, we have
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime\prime}
. This is captured by
{\tt{cond1}}
.

(2)

Consider
y\neq x
. If
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{w,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{w,y}
,
and we have
t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}^{y}t
. Then we add

(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{y}_{2}
. To get a cycle, we need a path from

t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
.

•

If
t^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{w,x}\cap\mathcal{T}^{w,y}
, then we add
(t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{x}_{2}
, resulting in a cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond2}}
.

•

If we have a path

t^{\prime\prime\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}]^{+}t^{\prime}
, then we get a cycle again. This is handled by
{\tt{cond3}}
.

•

As a last case, to obtain a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
,
assume there is a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t
in
\tau_{1}
, and let
t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{w,x}
be the last transaction writing to
x
in this path. Note that this will induce a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
: the path from
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
comes by the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{x}_{2}
edge added from
t^{\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
since
we have
(t^{\prime},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}^{x}
. Once we get this path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
, we again have the path we were looking for to get the cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond4}}
.

Thus, we have shown that obtaining a path from
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t^{\prime}
is covered
by the conditions
{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,{\tt{cond}_{4}}
, and hence,
a
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cycle.

(3)

Consider
y\neq x
. If
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{w,y}
such that
t_{4}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t^{\prime}
,
and we have
t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}^{y}t
. Then we add

(t_{4},t^{\prime\prime\prime})\in~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}~
. To get a cycle, we need a path from

t^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
t_{4}
.
If we have a path

t^{\prime\prime\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{1p}}~t_{4}
, then we get a cycle. This is handled by
{\tt{cond3}}
.

Thus, we can think of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2}}
cycles as a result of the forbidden patterns described in
{\tt{cond}_{i}}

1\leq i\leq 4
. By construction of
\tau_{1}

\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}

\tau_{2}
, we ensure
\neg{\tt{cond}_{1}},\dots,\neg{\tt{cond}_{4}}
. Hence,

\tau_{2}\models{\tt CM}
.
∎

Define
\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}
as the set of traces generated using
\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}
transitions, starting from an empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.

Consider a terminal trace
\tau
generated by
{\tt CM}
DPOR algorithm starting from the empty trace
\tau_{\emptyset}
.
That is, there is a sequence

\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}
with

\tau_{0}=\tau_{\emptyset}
,
and
\tau_{n}=\tau
.
Since
\tau_{\emptyset}
is a empty we have
\tau_{\emptyset}\models{\tt CM}
,
it follows by
Lemmas
C.2
and
C.3
that
\tau\models{\tt CM}
.

Hence, for each trace
\tau\in\big[[\sigma]\big]^{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}
we have
\tau\models{\tt CM}
.

C.3.
{\tt CM}
DPOR Completeness

In this section, we show the completeness of the DPOR algorithm. More precisely, for the input program under
{\tt CM}
for any terminating run
\rho\in Runs(\gamma)
and trace
\tau
s.t.
\rho\models\tau
,
we show that
\textsc{ExploreTraces}({\tt CM},\tau_{\emptyset},\epsilon)
will produce a recursive visit
\textsc{ExploreTraces}({\tt CM},\tau^{\prime},\pi)
for some terminal
\tau^{\prime}
, and
\pi
where
\tau^{\prime}=\tau
. First, we give some definitions and auxiliary lemmas.

Let
\pi=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
be an observation sequence where
\alpha_{t}=\mathsf{begin}(p,t)\dots

\mathtt{end}
(p,t)
.

\alpha_{t}
is called an
observable
, and is a sequence
of events from transaction
t
.
Given
\pi
and a trace
\tau
, we define

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi
to represent a sequence

\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{1}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{2}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\cdot\cdot\cdot\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{n}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}
, where

\tau_{0}=\tau
and

\pi=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
. Moreover, we define
\pi(\tau):=\tau_{n}
.

\tau
is
terminal
if there is no event left to execute
i.e

{\tt succ}\!\left(\tau\right)=\emptyset
.
We define
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi
to say that
(i)

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi
and
(ii)

\pi(\tau)
is terminal.

Definition C.4.

(
p
-free and
t
-free observation sequences)
For a process
p\in\mathbf{P}
, we say that an observation sequence
\pi
is
p
-free if all observables
\alpha_{t}
in
\pi
pertain to
transactions
t
not in
p
.
For a transaction
t
issued in
p
we say that
\pi
is
t
-free if
\pi
is
p
-free.

Definition C.5.

(Independent Observables)
For observables
\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we write
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
to represent they are independent.
This means

(i)

t_{1},t_{2}
are transactions issued in different processes, that is,

t_{1}
is issed in
p_{1}
,
t_{2}
is issued in
p_{2}
, with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
,

(ii)
no read event
r(p_{2},t_{2},x,v)
of transaction
t_{2}
reads from
t_{1}
, and

(iii)
no read event
r(p_{1},t_{1},x,v)
of transaction
t_{1}
reads from
t_{2}
. Thus,
\neg(\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}})
if either
t_{1},t_{2}
are issued in the same process, or there is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
relation between
t_{1},t_{2}
. That is,
\neg(\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}})
iff
t_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{2}
or
t_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t_{1}
.

Definition C.6.

(Independent Observation Sequences)
Observation sequences
\pi^{1},\pi^{2}
are called independent written
\pi^{1}\sim\pi^{2}
if there are observables
\alpha_{t_{1}}
,
\alpha_{t_{2}}
, and observation sequences
\pi^{\prime}

and
\pi^{\prime\prime}
such that
\pi^{1}=\pi^{\prime}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}
,

\pi^{2}=\pi^{\prime}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
.
In other words, we get
\pi^{2}
from
\pi^{1}
by swapping neighbouring independent observables corresponding to transactions
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
.

We use
\approx
to denote reflexive transitive closure of
\sim
.

Definition C.7.

(Equivalent Traces)
For traces
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\rangle
and
\tau_{2}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
,
we say
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
are equivalent denoted
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
if
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}

and for all
t_{1}
,
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{w,x}
for all variables
x
for each process
p
,
we have
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{1p}}^{x}
]
t_{2}
iff
t_{1}
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}^{x}
]
t_{2}
.

Lemma C.8.

((\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2})\wedge\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t})\Rightarrow(\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}\wedge(\alpha_{t}(\tau_{1})\equiv\alpha_{t}(\tau_{2})))
.

Proof.

Assume
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
for an observable
\alpha_{t}
, and

\tau_{1}=(\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1})
,

\tau_{2}=(\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2})
with
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
. Then we know that
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}
.

Since
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
, let
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau^{\prime}
.

\tau^{\prime}=(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime})
where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\{t\}
,

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup\{(t_{1},t)\mid t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
is in the same process as
t\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}
can contain
(t^{\prime},t)
for some

t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
, when
t^{\prime},t\in{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}^{w,x}
for some variable
x
, based
on the trace semantics. In particular if we have

t_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{2}
,
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x}
and
t_{3}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime})^{+}t_{2}
, then

t_{3}~{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}}^{x}~t_{1}
.
7
7

7

We write
t~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{x}~t^{\prime}
to denote that this
\\
hb{}
relation is added because of a read on
x
by a transaction
t^{\prime\prime}
in the trace semantics.

Since
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
, for any transactions
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
for all variables
x
. In particular,
t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}^{x}t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}^{x}t^{\prime\prime}
for all
x
.
Now, let us construct a trace
\tau^{\prime\prime}=(\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime\prime})
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime\prime}
is the smallest set such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime\prime}
and
whenever
(i)
t_{2}\in p
,
t_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{2}
,
\tau_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
and
t_{3}~({\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime})~t_{2}
, then
t_{3}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime\prime}}^{x}t_{1}

and (ii)
t_{2}\in p
,
t_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}}^{x}t_{2}
,
\tau_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}

and
t_{1}~({\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime})~t_{3}
,
then
t_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime\prime}}^{x}t_{3}
.

Since
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{1p}}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
iff
t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}^{x}]t^{\prime\prime}
for all
x
, and all
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime\prime}
is the smallest extension of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}
based
on the trace semantics, along with the fact that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime\prime}
,
we obtain for each process
p
, for any two
transactions
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
,

t_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime}]t_{2}
iff
t_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime\prime}]t_{2}
.
This gives
\tau^{\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}
.

This also gives
\tau_{2}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
,
that is,
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
and indeed
\alpha_{t}(\tau_{1})=\tau^{\prime}\equiv\tau^{\prime\prime}=\alpha_{t}(\tau_{2})
.

∎

Lemma C.9.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
and
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

Proof.

Let
\tau=\langle\mathcal{T},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}\rangle
be a trace and let
t_{1}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{1}
, and
t_{2}
be a transaction issued in process
p_{2}
, with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
. Assume
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}
, with
\alpha_{t_{1}}{\sim}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

We consider the following cases.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
. In this case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}
holds trivially.

(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)=(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}\rangle
,
where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
.

\bullet

t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
,
t_{1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
and
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
.
We know that
\tau_{1}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\rangle
, where

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{1}\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t,t_{1})|t\in p_{1}\}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
since there are no read events in
t_{1}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
since by the trace semantics, when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
remain same, there are no
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
edges to be added.

Consider
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.

Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{2}
,
reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get a sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.
Hence
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\{t_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{1}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{2}}\dots\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i_{n}})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i_{1}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i_{2}}\dots\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i_{n}})
.
Since
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
, for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},x_{i})
.

Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we know that for all
ev_{i}:1\leq i\leq n
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},x_{i})
. It follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.
Define
\tau_{3}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
, where
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\mathcal{T}\cup\{t_{2}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup\{(t^{\prime},t_{2})|t^{\prime}\in p_{2}\}
. It follows that
\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\tau_{3}
,
\tau_{3}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3})=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau))
.

\bullet

t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
and
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
. Similar to the previous case.

\bullet

t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}\cap\mathcal{T}^{wt}
.
Let
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}\rangle
and
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})=\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
.
Since
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{1}
has read events.
Let there be
n
read events
ev_{1}\dots ev_{n}
in transaction
t_{1}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{n}
on variables

x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
.
Hence we get the sequence
\tau\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{i_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{1},t^{\prime}_{n})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{i_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{1})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}
.
Since
t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, we know that
t_{2}
has read events.
Let there be
m
read events
ev^{\prime}_{1}\dots ev^{\prime}_{m}
in transaction
t_{2}
, reading from transactions
t^{\prime\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime\prime}_{m}
on variables

y_{1},\dots,y_{m}
.
Then we get the sequence
\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{begin}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{1})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{1}}\xrightarrow{}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\tau^{\prime}_{j_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t_{2},t^{\prime\prime}_{m})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{j_{n}}\dots\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}(t_{2})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{2}
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}}}

Since
\tau_{1}=\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
, for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
, we have
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
Since
\tau_{2}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau_{1})
, for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{1},t_{2},y_{j})
.
Since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
,
it follows that, we have
t^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{2},y_{j})
for all read events
ev^{\prime}_{j}
such that
ev^{\prime}_{j}.trans=t_{2}
. Hence
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}
.

Consider
\tau_{3}=\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau)=\langle\mathcal{T}_{3},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{3}\rangle
. To show that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
,
we show that for all read events
ev_{i}
such that
ev_{i}.trans=t_{1}
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. We prove this using contradiction.
Assume that
\exists t^{\prime}_{i}
s.t.
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
.

Let
p
be process of
t_{1}
and
pi
of
t_{2}
.
If
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, then
(1) there is a
t^{\prime}_{k}
such that
(i)
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
and (ii)
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{k}
,
or (2) there are transactions
t_{3}
and
t_{4}
such that
t_{4}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
,
t_{3}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}_{3}t_{1}
,

t_{4}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{i}
and
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t_{4}
,
or (3) there are transactions
t_{3}
and
t_{4}
such that
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x}
,
t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t_{1}
,

t_{3}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t_{4}
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},y}
.
or (4) there is a transaction
t_{3}
such that
t_{3}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x,y}
,
t_{3}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{y}_{3}t_{1}
,
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{wt},x,y}
.

Consider case (1).
If (i) is true, that is,
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
, since
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, we also have
t^{\prime}_{k}\in{\tt{check}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
.
This, combined with
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
, gives according to the trace semantics,
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{1}]t^{\prime}_{i}
. Going back to (ii),
we can have
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{k}
only if we observed one of the following:

(a)

There is a path from
t^{\prime}_{i}
to
t^{\prime}_{k}
in
\tau
; that is,

t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}]^{+}t^{\prime}_{k}
. This implies that
t^{\prime}_{i}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t_{1},x_{i})
which is a contradiction.

(b)

There is direct path from
t^{\prime}_{i}
to
t^{\prime}_{k}
in
\tau_{3}
due to the new
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p^{\prime}}}
edges added due to
t_{2}
.
But
t_{2}
has no successor in
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3})
and
p^{\prime}\neq p
.
So, we can have
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{k}
only if we have
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{k}
which is covered in above case.

A similar argument for other cases.

Thus, we have
\neg(t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}_{3}]t^{\prime}_{k})
. This, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{3},t_{1},x_{i})
. Thus, we now have
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\alpha_{t_{1}}
.
It remains to show that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
.

\mathbf{Proving}

\mathtt{(\alpha_{t_{1}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}{\cdot}\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)}

Define
\tau_{4}{=}\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau_{3}){=}\alpha_{t_{1}}.\alpha_{t_{2}}(\tau){=}\langle\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{4}\rangle
. Recall that

\tau_{2}{=}
\alpha_{t_{2}}.\alpha_{t_{1}}(\tau)
=
\langle\mathcal{T}_{2},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{2}\rangle
.

To show that
(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau)\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau)
, we show that
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
, and, for any two transactions

t^{\prime}_{k},t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4}
,
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
iff

t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{4p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
.
Of these, trivially,

\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{4}
follow. Since
\alpha_{t_{2}}\sim\alpha_{t_{1}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{4}
.

Since
p\neq p^{\prime}
and
t_{2}
has no successor in
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{3}
, it follows that
t^{\prime}_{k},t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{4}
,
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
iff

t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{4p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
.

The converse direction, that is, whenever
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{4p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
, we also have
t^{\prime}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{2p}}]t^{\prime}_{i}
is proved on similar lines.

∎

Lemma C.10.

If
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
,
\alpha_{t_{1}}\sim\alpha_{t_{2}}
, and
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})
, then (i)
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})
and (ii)
(\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}})(\tau_{1})\equiv(\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}})(\tau_{2})
.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
C.8
and Lemma
C.9
.
∎

From Lemma
C.10
, we get following lemma.

Lemma C.11.

If
\tau_{1}\equiv\tau_{2}
,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\sim\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
, and
\tau_{1}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
then
\tau_{2}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}(\tau_{1})\equiv\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}(\tau_{2})
.

Lemma C.12.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
,
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
then
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
for some
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
where
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is
t
-free.

Consider observables
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\alpha_{t_{1}},\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
.
We write
\pi_{\tt{T}}\lessapprox\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
to represent that
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{0}}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{1}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t_{2}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{n}}}
.
In other words,
\pi_{\tt{T}}
occurs as a non-contiguous subsequence in
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
. For such a
\pi_{\tt{T}},\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
,
we define
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\oslash\pi_{\tt{T}}:=\pi_{\tt{T^{0}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\dots\pi_{\tt{T^{n}}}
.
Since elements of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
are distinct, operation
\oslash
is well defined.
Let
\pi_{\tt{T}}[i]
denote the
i
th observable
in the observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T}}
, and
let
|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
denote the number of observables
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Let
\alpha_{t}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[i]
for some
i:1\leq i\leq|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
.
We define
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
as a subsequence
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
such that
(i)
\alpha_{t}\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
,
(ii)
\alpha_{t_{j}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[j]\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
for some
j:1\leq j<i
iff there exists
k:j<k\leq i
such that
\alpha_{t_{k}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}[k]\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
and
\neg(\alpha_{t_{j}}\sim\alpha_{t_{k}})
.
Thus,
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
consists of
\alpha_{t}
and all

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}
appearing before
\alpha_{t}
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
such that

t^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t
.

Lemma C.13.

If
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}=\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
and
\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}=\pi_{\tt{T}}\oslash\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
then
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
.

Proof.

The proof is trivial since we can always execute in order,
the
~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~
predecessors of
\alpha_{t}
from
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
, then
\alpha_{t}
, then the observables
in
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
which are independent from
\alpha_{t}
, followed by the suffix of
\pi_{\tt{T}}
after
\alpha_{t}
.

∎

Lemma C.14.

If
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\cdot\pi^{\prime\prime}_{T}
and
\alpha_{t}\in\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
then
\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}=\tt{Pre(\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}},\alpha_{t})}
.

Proof.

The proof is trivial once again, since all
the transactions which are
~{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}~t
are in the prefix
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
.
∎

Lemma C.15.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
.

Proof.

This simply follows from the fact that we can extend the observation sequence

\pi_{\tt{T}}
to obtain a terminal configuration, since the
{\tt CM}
DPOR algorithm generates traces corresponding to terminating runs. ∎

Lemma C.16.

Consider a trace trace
\tau
such that
\tau\models{\tt CM}
,
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\pi_{\tt{T}}\cdot\alpha_{t}
.
Let each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
in
\alpha_{t}
read from some transaction
t_{i}
,
and let
\pi_{\tt{T}}
be
t
-free.
Then
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T}}
, where
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
is the same as
\alpha_{t}
,
with the exception that the sources of its read events can be different. That is,
each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
can read from some transaction
t^{\prime}_{i}\neq t_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\alpha_{t_{1}}\alpha_{t_{2}}\dots\alpha_{t_{n}}
and
\tau_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{1}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{2}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\dots\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t_{n}}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{t}}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}\tau_{n+1}
, where
\tau_{0}=\tau
. Let
\tau_{i}=\langle\mathcal{T}_{i},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i}\rangle
.
Let there be
m
read events in transaction
t
.
Let
p
be process
t
.
Keeping in mind what we want to prove, where we want to execute
t
first followed by
\pi_{\tt{T}}
, and obtain an execution
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
, we do the following.

For each read event
ev_{i}=r_{i}(p,t,x_{i},v)
we define
t^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt,x_{i}}
such that

(i)

t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
, and

(ii)
there is no
t^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})

where
t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{np}}]t^{\prime\prime}_{i}
. Note that this is possible since

\pi_{\tt{T}}
is
t
-free, so all the observables
\alpha_{t_{i}}
occurring in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
are such that
t_{i}
is issued in a process other than that of
t
. Thus, when
\alpha_{t}
is enabled, we can choose any of the
writes done earlier, this fact is consistent with the
{\tt CM}
semantics, since from Lemmas
C.2
-
C.3
we know
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CM}
.
Since
\tau_{n}\models{\tt CM}
such a
t^{\prime}_{i}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{0},t,x_{i})
exists for each
ev_{i}
.

Next we define a sequence of traces which can give the execution

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
Define a sequence of traces
\tau^{\prime}_{0},\tau^{\prime}_{1},\dots\tau^{\prime}_{n}
where
\tau^{\prime}_{j}=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{j}\rangle
is such that

(1)

\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,

(2)

\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for all
j:0\leq j\leq n
.

•

Define
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{i}\cup\{t\}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

Define
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}=\{t^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}]t\mid t^{\prime}
is a transaction in
\tau
, in the same process as
t\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i\leq n
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i:1\leq i\leq m}t^{\prime}_{i}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{x_{i}}]t
(reads from relation corresponding to each read event
ev_{i}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{t}
), and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j:1\leq j\leq m}{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime}_{j}
(each
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime}_{j}
corresponds to the updated
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
relation because of the read transition
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{read}(t,t^{\prime}_{j})}_{{\tt CM}{-}{\tt tr}}
).

For
1\leq i\leq n
, we define
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i}
inductively as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}

and show that
\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
holds good.
First define
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{0}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}^{\prime}
.
Then define
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i}
to be the coherence order corresponding to
\alpha_{t_{i}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i-1})

where
\tau^{\prime}_{i-1}=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{{i-1}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i-1}\rangle

for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
.

Base case
. The base case
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}{\alpha^{\prime}_{t}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
,
holds trivially, by construction.

Inductive hypothesis
. Assume that
\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
for
0\leq j\leq i-1
.

We have to prove that

\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{i+1}=\alpha_{t_{i+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i})
.

(1)

If
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{wt}
and
t_{i+1}\notin\mathcal{T}^{rd}
, then the proof holds trivially from the inductive hypothesis.

(2)

Consider now
t_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}^{rd}
. Consider a read event

ev^{i+1}=r(p,t_{i+1},x,v)\in\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
which was reading from
transaction
t_{x}
in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
. That is, we had
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
. If
t_{x}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, then we are done, since we can simply extend the run from the inductive hypothesis.

Assume otherwise. That is,
t_{x}\notin{\tt{readable}}(\tau^{\prime}_{i},t_{i+1},x)
.

Since
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
, there are some blocking transactions in the new path, which prevents
t_{x}
from being readable.
We have the blocking transactions since
t
is moved before
\pi_{\tt{T}}
. But
t
updates only
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
and for each process of
t_{i+1}
is not equal
p
.
And
t
has no successor in each
[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}^{\prime}_{j}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}}^{\prime}_{j}]^{+}
.

Hence,
t_{x}

\in

{\tt{readable}}(\tau_{i},t_{i+1},x)
and we can extend the run
from the inductive hypothesis obtaining
\tau^{\prime}_{j}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{j+1}}
,
\tau^{\prime}_{j+1}=\alpha_{t_{j+1}}(\tau^{\prime}_{j})
,
for
0\leq j\leq i-1
,
and

\tau^{\prime}_{i}\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t_{i+1}}
.

Now we prove that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i+1}={\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i+1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}
.
Assume we have
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i+1}
.
We show that
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
Let
p1\neq p
be the process such that
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
is because of
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p1}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
We have the following cases:

(i)

(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i}
. From the inductive hypothesis it follows that
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i}
and hence in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.

(ii)

(tr1,tr2)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i}
.
Since
(tr1,tr2)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}_{i}
and
(tr1,tr2)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}}^{\prime}_{i}
, it follows that moving
t
before
\pi_{\tt{T}}
caused this.
But
t
only updates
{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}
edges and
p1=p
.
Since
\pi_{\tt{T}}
be
t
-free, we get a contradiction.

Thus, we have shown that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}

is such that
\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\alpha_{t_{1}}\dots\alpha_{t_{i}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{i}
for all

0\leq i\leq n
. When
i=n
we obtain

\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}_{n}
, or
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
∎

Definition C.17 (Linearization of a Trace).

A observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T}}
is a
linearization
of a trace
\tau=\langle\mathcal{T},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\mathsf{HB}_{p}}\rangle
if
(i)

\pi_{\tt{T}}
has the same transactions as
\tau
and
(ii)

\pi_{\tt{T}}
follows the (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}po}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\it{rf}})
relation.

We say that our DPOR algorithm
generates an observation sequence

\pi_{\tt{T}}
from state
\tau
where
\tau
is a trace
if
it invokes
ExploreTraces
with parameters
{\tt CM},\tau,\pi^{\prime}
, where
\pi^{\prime}
is a
linearization
of
\tau
, and generates a sequence of recursive calls to
ExploreTraces
resulting in
\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Lemma C.18.

If
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
then,
the DPOR algorithm generates
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}
from state
\tau

for some
\pi^{\prime}_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T}}
.

Proof.

We use induction on
|\pi_{\tt{T}}|
. If
\tau
is
terminal
, then the proof is trivial.
Assume that we have
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
.
Assume that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
. It follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha_{t}
.
Using Lemma
C.12
we get
\pi_{\tt{T}}=\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{2}}}
, where
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is
t
-free.
We consider the following two cases:

•

In the first case, we assume that all read events in
t
read from
transactions in
\tau
. So in this case,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
can be empty.
Assume there are
n
read events in
t
and each read event
ev_{i}=r(p,t,x_{i},v)

reads from transactions
t_{i}\in\tau
for all
i:1\leq i\leq n
.
In this case the DPOR algorithm will let each read event
ev_{i}
read from all possible transactions
t^{\prime}\in{\tt{readable}}(\tau,t,x_{i})
, including
t_{i}
.

•

In the second case, assume that there exists at least one read event
ev^{\prime}=r(p,t,x,v)\in\alpha_{t}
which reads from a transaction
t^{\prime}\in\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
(hence,
\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
is non empty).

From Lemma
C.16
, we know that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}
.
From Lemma
C.15
, it follows that
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\alpha^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
, for some

\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.
Hence there exists
\tau^{\prime}
such that

\tau^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}_{t}(\tau)
and
\tau^{\prime}\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.

Since
|\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}|<|\pi_{\tt{T}}
, we can use the inductive hypothesis. It follows that the DPOR algorithm generates from state
\tau^{\prime}
,
the observation sequence
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}
such that
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
.

Let
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}.\alpha_{t}=\tt{Pre(\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}},\alpha_{t})}
. Then
\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{1}}}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{4}}}
implies that
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\lessapprox\pi_{\tt{T^{5}}}
(by applying Lemma
C.14
).
Let
\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T}}\oslash(\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t})
.
By applying Lemma
C.13
, we get
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
Since
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T}}
and

\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
,
by applying Lemma
C.10
, we get
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
Let
\tau^{\prime}=(\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t})(\tau)
.
Since
\tau\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
, we have
\tau^{\prime}\vdash_{\tt{G_{T}}}\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.
From inductive hypothesis it follows that
\tau^{\prime}
generates
\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
such that
\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{6}}}
.

In other words,
\tau
generates
\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
where
\pi_{\tt{T}}\approx\pi_{\tt{T^{3}}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\cdot\pi_{\tt{T^{7}}}
.

∎

\tt{Cond1}
